10.2  Torque ^te000221

Rotational analog of force: how effective a force is at changing rotation. It depends on F, on where the force acts
(r) and on its direction (θ). ^te000222

Connections: becomes the "force" in τ = Iα (10.3) and does work when it acts through an angle (10.4).  Vector form: Ch. 11. ^te000223

Torque τ ^te000226

T3 ^te000228

τ = rF sin θ   (10.10) ^te000229

r: from the rotation axis to the point where the force is
applied.   θ: angle between r and F. ^te000230

SI unit: N·m  (same unit as energy, but the joule is reserved for
energy). ^te000231

Direction: clockwise or counterclockwise. ^te000232

Condition: specify the rotation axis first. The same force gives
different torques about different axes. ^te000233

10.3  Rotational Inertia and the Analog of Newton's Law ^te000316

Chapter 10 ^te001639

Rotational Motion ^te001640

Rotation of a rigid body about a fixed axis = one-dimensional motion written with angles.
Every linear quantity has a rotational analog, and v = ωr ties the two descriptions together. ^te001641

EUP 4th ed. (Global Edition), pp. 191–211 ^te001642
